Water Temple dungeon walkthrough in Ocarina of Time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Water Temple is a dungeon located at the bottom of Lake Hylia in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake, and it's only reachable after Link has traveled into the future.

This infamous dungeon is fairly complicated, as you need to constantly adjust the water level inside to unlock more rooms and items. However, as the water level changes, you'll need to constantly adjust your depth to find more rooms. It's a surprise Link doesn't suffer from decompression sickness.

To save Link from a headache, here's how to complete the Water Temple in Ocarina of Time remake.

Update (Nov. 9): We've updated this guide with the alternate route through the Water Temple for players who don't have the Scarecrow Song.

To get inside the Water Temple, you'll first need to get the Iron Boots from the Ice Cavern and the Zora Tunic from King Zora. Then, approach the island with the large tree on it from the northeast. You'll find the remaining water in Lake Hylia and an underwater door. Equip the Iron Boots and walk into the water. Look above the door to see a target, and Hookshot it to open the door to the Water Temple.

Walk inside the Water Temple, and take off your Iron Boots to float to the surface. Walk into the main room ahead, and defeat the Blue Tektite on the middle platform. Then, put the Iron Boots back on and descend to the floor. There will be Spikes roaming around, and they're only vulnerable when their spikes are retracted. You can't use your sword underwater, so use your Hookshot to hit them. Go into the doorway on the east side of the room, where you'll find Princess Ruto.

After you talk to Ruto, take off the Iron Boots to float to the surface. Open the chest to receive the dungeon map. Then, stand in front of the crest, and play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A). This will lower the water level in the hole behind you.

Drop into the hole and make sure to land in the water. With the water gone, the torch near the entrance is now lit. Stand in the doorway, and look toward the lit torch. Then, aim your Fairy Bow, so that your arrow will go through the fire, and hit one of the unlit torches along the wall. Alternatively, if you unlocked the Great Fairy Fountain at the top of Death Mountain, you can use Din's Fire. Light both torches to unlock the door, and head inside.

There will be three Spikes in this room. Roll away from them, and then hit them when their spikes are retracted. After you defeat them, a chest with a small key will appear. Open it and return to the main room.

Break the rock wall to the south, and use your Iron Boots to sink to the bottom of the water. A Shell Blade will be waiting for you at the end of the hall. Quickly hit the inside of their mouth with your Hookshot to defeat them. If you miss, make sure to roll out of the way and try again.

Walk to the end of the hall, and take off your Iron Boots to float to the surface. Climb onto the platform, and step on the button on the second tier. A statue with a target will rise from the ground. Hookshot over to it, and defeat the Blue Tektites up ahead. There's also a Like Like on the higher platform. Rather than Hookshotting to the target, Hookshot to the Like Like to stun them. Then, you can unload a ton of attacks on them before they try to suck you up. Avoid their attack and then go in for the final few swings. Then, open the chest to get the compass.

Return to the main room, and this time, head to the east. Push the block until it drops into water. Then, put on your Iron Boots and drop in. A Shell Blade will be waiting for you in the water, so be ready to hit the inside of their mouth with your Hookshot. After you defeat them, walk to the north and take off your Iron Boots to float to the top.

In the next room, there will be a whirlpool. If you look in the water, you'll see a platform about halfway down. Put on your Iron Boots and float to the platform. Then, hit the object in the statue's mouth with your Hookshot. This will open a gate underwater. Take off your Iron Boots, dash through the gate, and quickly dash to the surface. There are two Shell Blades, but you can avoid them if you quickly get out of the water. Open the chest to get a small key.

Return to the main room, and use a small key to open the door on the pillar in front of you. Once inside, turn around and look up to see a target that you can Hookshot too. You'll find the royal crest, so you know what that means. Play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) in front of it, which will raise the water level.

You can't reach the Hookshot target on the ceiling just yet, so instead, put on the Iron Boots and sink into the hole that the cube was blocking. Down here, you'll encounter three Spikes. Defeat them with your Hookshot, and then Hookshot the object on the wall. A hole in the ceiling will open up, but a Shell Blade will also come with it. Wait for it to open its mouth, and then Hookshot its insides.

Float into the hole in the ceiling. In this room, you'll find a red rupee and a Gold Skulltula on the west wall. Return to the area with the crest, and go through the door beside the floating cube. This will spit you out in the main room, but now the water level should be at the second floor.

Head to the east and break the rock wall. In this next area, there are Stingers in the water, so be ready to hit them with your Hookshot! After you defeat them, walk to the end of the path and Hookshot to the target above. In this room, there will be a pillar of water and a diamond-shaped object submerged in a pool next to it. Hit the diamond-shaped object, and the pillar of water will lower. Stand on the platform, and hit the diamond-shaped object again to ride the water up. Jump over to the hallway, and open the chest inside to get a small key.

Return to the main room, but this time, put on your Iron Boots and sink to the lowest level. Go into the east room again, and float to the top to find a rock wall. Break it, and you'll enter a room with three Shell Blades and a Gold Skulltula. Defeat the enemies, get the Gold Skulltula token, and then head back to the main room.

Unlock the west door, and hit the diamond-shaped object inside. A Blue Tektite will ride the water down. Defeat it, stand on the platform, and then hit the diamond-shaped object with a ranged weapon like your Hookshot or Fairy Bow. You'll rise to find another royal crest. Play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) on the platform, and the water level will rise again.

Return to the main room, but don't walk off the balcony. Play the Scarecrow Song, which will summon Pierre onto the balcony. Pierre will speed up the process later on, so now you're free to unlock the west door. In this room, you need to quickly Hookshot from platform to platform until you reach the top. Walk to the edge in front of you, and drop onto the platform below. Then, look up and Hookshot to the moving platform in front of you. Then, keep Hookshotting to the higher platforms until you make it to the top.

Head through the door and hit the diamond-shaped object. Statues with targets around the room will rise, and so will the water level, bringing a few Blue Tektites with it. Defeat the enemies, and then Hookshot over to the statue to your east. Hit the diamond-shaped object to lower the statues, and then Hookshot to the target on the wall to the north.

Climb over the statue's head, and hit the diamond-shaped object. Then, jump onto the platform on the statue's head, and hit the diamond-shaped object again. After you ride the platform up, go through the door to the north.

The next room is full of fog. Head straight ahead and go past the tree. Walk up to the door, but it'll lock itself. If you turn around, you'll see a shadowy figure standing beside the tree. If you walk up to it, you'll realize it's actually Dark Link!

There's one important thing to note when fighting Dark Link: don't attack him while his shield is up. He'll jump onto your sword and absolutely mog you. Early on in the fight, he'll just jump off, but later on he'll hit you. It's also just one of the coolest things you'll see in all of Ocarina of Time, so if you do want to see it, do it early on in the fight.

There are two phases to the Dark Link fight. For the first part, he'll slash his sword three times, with the third being the most powerful. Make sure to side jump or flip away a couple of times to avoid the third attack, and then get in a few swings of your own. Repeat this a few times until Dark Link submerges himself in the water.

When he resurfaces, he'll incorporate two new attacks: a jump attack and a spin attack. For the jump attack, Dark Link will swing twice and then do a side jump. Wait for him to jump, flip away, and then go in for your own attack. Later on in the fight, Dark Link will start to jump attack without any warning, so be ready to dodge as soon as he jumps. For the spin attack, he'll swing twice, stand in place, and then charge up his sword before unleashing the spin attack. Back away, wait for the spin attack to finish, and then go in for an attack. Like the jump attack, Dark Link will just go straight into the spin attack later on in the fight, so get ready to back away.

After you defeat Dark Link, the door to the north will open. Head on through and open the chest to get the Longshot. This will upgrade your Hookshot, letting you reach targets twice as far away.

Longshot to one of the targets above, and drop into the water below. Follow the water current, but avoid the whirlpools. You can either dash away from them or equip your Iron Boots and walk by them if you want to be safe.

After the first corner, there will be a short turn coming up, but there is a Gold Skulltula on the wall nearby. Equip the Iron Boots after the first corner, and walk to the turn. Look up to the north wall, and you'll see the Gold Skulltula. Longshot it to retrieve its token.

Climb onto the ground just before the final whirlpool, and hit the eye on the wall above the gate. Quickly Longshot to the target before the gate closes, and head through the door to the south. Drop into the water, which will put you in the east room on the lowest floor. Follow the path to return to the main room.

Put on your Iron Boots and go back to the east room on the lowest floor. Float to the top and use Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) to lower the water level. Then, go into the central pillar, and Longshot to the second floor. Play Zelda's Lullaby at the crest to raise the water level, and then Longshot to the ceiling to get the Gold Skulltula.

Exit the door, and go to the floating cube on the north side of the pillar. Longshot the eye on the wall, and then Longshot the target to pull yourself in. Go to the northeast corner and Longshot the target above the waterfall.

Equip your Iron Boots and follow the path until you find a stone cube on your right. Pull the cube out until you can't pull it anymore, and then Longshot onto the target again. This time, push the cube until you can't anymore. Go into the opening in the wall and float to the surface. Open the chest at the top of the stairs to get a small key.

Return to the main room, and sink to the lowest level. Go into the northern doorway, and Longshot to the target at the end of the hall. Open the door, and you'll enter a room with a whirpool that is full of Blue Tektites. Get rid of the Blue Tektites with your Fairy Bow, as they'll be pretty annoying if you don't.

Additionally, there are two holes in the wall: one in the northwest corner and one in the southeast corner. Rolling boulders will fall out of these holes. Wait for the one in the southeast to pass, and then drop into the water. Swim over to the north side of the room, and then put on your Iron Boots. Wait for the northeastern boulder to roll by, walk up to the wall, and float to the top. You should grab the ledge and pull yourself up.

Go into the next room, which has three rock walls and a pool full of Stingers. There's also a button at the bottom of the pool that will raise the water level as long as it's being pressed. For now, break the middle rock wall, and pull the cube out. Then, break either the left or right rock wall, go behind the cube, and push it into the water.

After the water level rises, swim over to the doorway and follow the path. Take out the two Blue Tektites with your bow and then step onto the button. Jump across the three platforms and go through the door on the other side. This will bring you to the southeastern corner with the rolling boulders.

Wait for a boulder to pass, walk through the waterfall, and jump into the opening on your left. A Shell Blade will be waiting for you, so take them out or just run into the door behind them. Inside the next room, you'll find a chest with the boss key.

Before you sprint back to the main room, exit the room and look through the waterfall on your right. There will be a Gold Skulltula hiding near the ceiling. Use your Longshot to retrieve its token.

Return to the main room, and if you used the Scarecrow Song earlier, Longshot to the scarecrow in the southwest corner. If you don't have the Scarecrow Song, you need to go back through the door to the west. Hit the switch to lower the water, step onto the platform, and hit the switch again to go up to the next floor. Then, play Zelda's Lullaby at the crest to raise the water level. Go back to the main room and Longshot to the target in the north. Go through the door, and put on your Iron Boots to fight against the current. Wait for the Blade Traps to go by, and then walk to the top to find the boss room door leading to the Giant Aquatic Amoeba, Morpha.

Before you start the fight, it's important to note that Morpha is actually the orb floating around in the water. To damage them, you'll need to pull them out of the water with your Longshot. Additionally, Morpha will go back into the water after every attack. Some of their attacks will sprout multiple tentacles out of the water, but only one of them will be real. Look in the water and watch them as they swim around. Where they end up will be where their real tentacle appears.

When you're ready to take on Giant Aquatic Amoeba Morpha, hop onto the floating platforms in the middle of the room. For the first part of the fight, Morpha will sprout out of the water, slam the ground three times, and then rest for a bit. Use your Longshot to pull them out of the water while they're resting, and unload as many attacks as you can.

Then, Morpha will come out of the water and do a spin attack. We recommend running around the outskirts of the arena during this part. Once they stop spinning, pull them with your Longshot and attack them.

Morpha will go back into the water, and a bunch of water tentacles will appear. Only one of them will contain Morpha's body. Find the correct tentacle and move over to it, but if you get near a tentacle, it will slam onto the ground. This is a great time to Longshot to the targets on the wall to get to Morpha as quickly as possible. Approach the real tentacle, and wait for it to slam the ground. Pull them out, and hit them as many times as you can.

For the next attack, three tentacles will come out of the water, and you guessed it, only one will contain Morpha's real body. However, this time, you won't have to chase it down, as it'll do the spin attack again and come for you. Like always, pull them out with Longshot and attack them. If you've been spam attacking Morpha, there's a good chance that the fight will end here.

If the fight didn't end, Morpha has one more attack up its sleeve. A wall of tentacles will come out of the water, and they'll all slam the ground at once. Look for Morpha's body, and attack it until you defeat them.

After you defeat them, collect the Heart Container and exit the dungeon through the ring of light.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is a little bit different than the original game, so if you need guides to help you through, we don’t blame you. We have walkthroughs for all the dungeons, from the Deku Tree to Ganondorf's Castle. We also have guides for the sillier, in-between parts of the game, like when you have to find Richard (the dog) or Cuccos.

For those going for 100% completion, we also have guides on where to find every Piece of Heart, every Gold Skulltula token, and where to plant every Magic Bean.

Spirit Temple dungeon walkthrough in Ocarina of Time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Travel through time to get through this dungeon

The Spirit Temple is another dungeon that you take on in the future in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake. You're sent to the Spirit Temple to find for Nabooru, the Gerudo's exalted leader.

However, when you get to the Spirit Temple, you quickly learn that you'll have to literally manage your time properly if you want to solve its puzzles and find Nabooru. The Spirit Temple is the first dungeon that you need to go to in the past and the future to reach its end.

Here's how to complete the Spirit Temple dungeon in Ocarina of Time remake.

Before you make your way to the Spirit Temple, we recommend learning the Scarecrow Song and bringing a magic bean and a bug if you want to collect every Gold Skulltula inside and in the surrounding area.

To get to the Spirit Temple, follow the flags in the Mirage Desert until you reach a building. Before you go up the stairs, go around the stairs and drop into the hole. You'll find a Gold Skulltula on the wall. Go back up to the top and read the plaque, which states, "Those with eyes of truth shall be guided to the Spirit Temple by a welcoming specter." Put on the Lenses of Truth, and you'll see the Phantom Guide. Tell them you're ready to go, and follow them closely. The Phantom Guide is a little annoying as they'll take many useless sharp turns, and they'll run through Leevers, which look like cactus monsters. You'll have to stay as close as you can to the phantom and avoid every incoming enemy until you eventually reach the entrance to the Evil Desert Colossus.

Then, head straight and attempt to go into the Spirit Temple. Sheik will stop you and essentially tell you that you need to go against the flow of time to complete the dungeon. They'll also teach you the Requiem of Spirit (L - R - L - A - R - L), which allows you to warp back to the Spirit Temple. So, play the Prelude of Light (X - A - X - A - Y - X) to go back to the Temple of Time, turn back into young Link, and warp back to the Spirit Temple.

Before you head inside, make sure to place a bug on the soft soil on the left side of the Spirit Temple's entrance to get a Gold Skulltula, and then plant a magic bean into it for when you travel back into the future.

Head up the left staircase, and you'll meet Nabooru just in front of a tunnel. She'll tell you that there's a treasure hidden behind the tunnel that can move the huge stones blocking the way on the other side of the temple.

Crawl through the tunnel, and you'll enter a room with a bunch of Keese, Blade Traps, and an Armos. Defeat all the enemies to unlock the two gates. First, follow the path to the left. In this room, you'll face off against a Stalfos, which will take a bit more to beat as your young self. After you defeat them, look to the north, and you'll see a glowing switch behind a wall. Throw your Boomerang to the left side of the wall, and then look to the right side of the wall to direct the Boomerang into the switch. When you hit it, the wall will fall and turn into a bridge. However, a couple of Green Bubbles will also spawn. After you take them out, head to the chest in the corner, but be careful as the pots are actually Flying Pots. Open the chest to get a red rupee and then crawl through the tunnel into the next room.

In this room, Anubis, a floating statue, will be on the opposite side of the room, and its movements will mirror yours as you walk around and attack. To defeat it, you need to hit the eye switch on the northwest pillar, and then jump onto it so that Anubis is in the fire on the other pillar. Make sure to jump from the center of the room though, or else you'll fall into the abyss. After you defeat Anubis, head through the unlocked door.

For this room, you need to collect the five crystals to unlock the door. However, there will be a Wallmaster and a Fire Keese that try to attack you as soon as you walk in. Take out the Keese first, and get out of the spotlight to attack the Wallmaster. Once they've been defeated, grab the crystals on the ground. Then, climb up the walls to grab the crystals in the air.

The wall will drop, but be careful of the Keese on the other side of the wall. Take a step onto the bridge to make the Keese get off the wall, and then take them out with your Fairy Slingshot or Boomerang. There's also a Gold Skulltula on the left wall. Walk onto the bridge and hit it with your Boomerang to collect its token.

Light a Deku Stick with one of the torches, and use it to light the unlit torches. A chest containing a small key will drop from the ceiling. Collect it and go through the door to the south to end up back in the room you started in. Crawl through the tunnel, and use the small key to unlock the door.

Use your Boomerang to hit the Skullwalltulas and climb up the wall. A couple Lizalfos will spawn in the room at the top. Defeat them, and then look at the western wall to see a breakable rock wall. Use a Bombchu to break the wall. If you don't have any, hit the switch to the south, and a chest containing five Bombchus will drop from the ceiling. Light will beam onto the sun on the floor, and the door to the next room will unlock.

In this room, there is a giant statue of a woman with a snake on her head. You'll find a lit torch and an unlit torch in front of her, and a Gold Skulltula on her right. Light the unlit torch, and a chest containing the dungeon map will spawn. Then, collect the Gold Skulltula token.

Climb to the top of the stairs, where you'll find a button and a statue on the platform above it. Climb up the wall and push the statue onto the button to unlock the door.

Head inside and go up the stairs. You'll enter another room where you must collect five crystals to move on, but this time there are a few Beamos hiding about. Blow up the Beamos with your bombs, and then you'll be free to start collecting crystals. First, climb onto the walls around the room to get four crystals. For the last crystal, climb onto the wall in the center of the room, and jump into it. The door to the next room will unlock, but don't head into it just yet.

Pull the cube with the sun on it into the beam of light, and a chest will spawn in the hole in the wall. Open it to get a small key. Now you're free to go into the next room, but immediately turn around to find a Gold Skulltula above the door. Use your Boomerang to collect its token.

Unlock the next room and head inside. You'll see an Iron Knuckle, which looks like a knight with a greataxe, sitting in a chair at the other end of the room, and yes, you need to defeat them to get to the next room. Get close to them, and they'll stand up. Your shield isn't that effective at blocking their attacks, so instead, you'll need to dodge. Because their attacks are so strong, they're also slow to recover, which is a great time to attack. They'll either slam the ground in front of them or sweep their axe to both sides. Backflip away from their attack, and then do a jump attack of your own to close the distance. If you're low on health, bait their attack into the stone pillars. Three hearts will fall from each one, so you can heal enough hearts to take one of their hits. Repeat this until you defeat them.

Open the door to the next room, and go down the path which will take you outside. Open the chest on your left, and you'll get the Silver Gauntlets, the treasure that Nabooru was looking for. Nabooru will greet you outside, but she'll shortly be attacked by Kotake and Koume. As the Silver Gauntlets can only be used by an adult, you'll now have to warp back to the Temple of Time, and return as an older Link seven years in the future. However, after you teleport, Kaepora Gaebora will greet you outside. They've come to say goodbye, as this is the final time you'll see them.

Travel to the future and return to the Spirit Temple by playing Requiem of Spirit (L - R - L - A - R - L). If you planted the magic bean as young Link, you can ride the plant to get a Gold Skulltula and a Piece of Heart.

Go up the right staircase in the Spirit Temple and push the black stone blocking the doorway on the right. Then, hit the switch on the ceiling to unlock the doors.

Head into the room on the left and a Wolfos will appear. Defeat them, and stand on the Triforce on the floor. Play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) and a chest will spawn on the platform ahead of you. Longshot to the target below it, and open the chest to get the compass.

Go back into the previous room, and take the path to the right. In this room, there are a bunch of rolling stones, and you need to collect five crystals to unlock the next room. You'll need to jump for the first crystal, but the other crystals can be found in the small alcoves. Before you play the Song of Time to get rid of the time cube blocking the way to one of the crystals, go into the last alcove on the left to find a Gold Skulltula. Then, play the Song of Time, which will move the time cube into the last alcove, but also reveal the final crystal.

Head into the unlocked room, where you'll find a chest and a Like Like. Defeat the Like Like and open the chest to get a small key. Backtrack into the room with the switch and Blade Trap, and use the small key to unlock the door. Two Flying Pots will charge at you and another Like Like will try to eat you. Defeat them and climb up the wall. At the top, quickly put on your Lenses of Truth to spot the invisible Floormaster. Wait for them to run into your shield or the wall, and then attack them and the hands that they split into.

Turn the light into the sun on the eastern wall, and a chest will fall from the ceiling. Then, skip the next sun, as it'll spawn a Wallmaster, and turn the light toward the sun on the southern wall to unlock the door. You can also skip the last sun, as it'll spawn a fake chest.

Head into the next room, which will be the room with the large statue of a woman who has a snake on her head. First, Longshot onto the statue's left wrist (your right). Step onto the Triforce on the hand, and play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A), which will spawn a chest onto the statue's right hand.

Before you head over, Longshot to the target in the northeast corner of the room, and hit the button with the Megaton Hammer to unlock the door. Then, drop back down to the floor, but don't immediately go to the door.

This time, Longshot onto the statue's right wrist (your left). Open the chest to get a small key, and then play the Scarecrow Song, which will summon Pierre onto the platform above you. Longshot over, and you'll find a Gold Skulltula on the floor. Collect it and drop back down.

Go through the door in front of the statue, and push the large stone block until it falls. Follow the path and do the same with the next stone block, which will create a path back to the starting room.

Head back to the room with the statue, and Longshot to the target above the door in the southeast corner of the room. Go up the stairs and use the small key to unlock the door, but be careful as there will be a Beamos waiting for you in the next room. Throw a bomb at them and head into the next room, where you'll find three Anubis and a Beamos.

First things first, take out the Beamos with a bomb. Then, go to the far side of the room and play the Song of Time (A - L - R - A - L - R) in front of the symbol on the wall. Next, step on the button in the northeast, which will turn on the fire pit. Direct every Anubis into the fire by jumping over to the time cube and walking to the northwest and southwest corners of the room.

Head into the unlocked room, where you'll find a button on the floor and an Armos on one side, and a beam of light on the other. You can't do anything with the sunlight for now, so stick to the side with the button. The Armos will be a monster, so instead of pulling them onto the button, you need to make them jump onto the button. This will turn them back into a statue and unlock the southern door.

Open the door, and use your Lenses of Truth to open the two chests in the hallway to get some rupees. Then, head into the next room, where you'll find another Iron Knuckle, but this time, you're older and stronger. Take them out like you did before, and remember that you can bait their attacks into the pillars to spawn hearts. However, only one heart will drop from each pillar this time.

After you defeat him, go into the next room and follow the path which will take you outside. Step on the button to spawn a chest, and open it to get the Mirror Shield.

Equip the Mirror Shield and return to the room with the sunlight. Hold ZL to lift your shield and step into the sunlight. Reflect it into the sun on the wall above the door to unlock it. Head inside and open the chest to get a small key. Go back to the room where you defeated the Anubis, and use the key to unlock the door.

Blow up the two Beamos, and head inside. You need to climb up the wall, but it'll be shifting from side to side. Use your Longshot on the highest wall and climb up the rest of the way. Two Lizalfos will appear at the top. Take care of them, and Longshot to the tap of the wall like you did the previous one.

Go into the next room, and play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) on the Triforce on the floor to unlock the door. Inside, you'll find a bunch of Torch Slugs and a bunch of fake doors. Use your Megaton Hammer to flip the slugs and defeat them. Then, break the door in the northwest corner with your hammer, and you'll find an eye behind it.

Hit the eye with your Longshot or bow, and a wall will fall over, creating a platform. Longshot to the target on the ceiling, and then step on the button to turn off the fire surrounding the chest. Longshot back over, and open the chest to get the boss key.

Return to the previous room and climb up the wall. Go through the door, but be weary of all the pots in this room as they're all Flying Pots. Drop onto the floor and hit the switch to unlock the door above. Open the door, and you'll find a beam of light and a sun on the wall. If you're low on bombs, reflect the light into the sun to spawn a chest that'll give you five.

Defeat the White Bubbles in the next room, and destroy the rock wall. There will be another reflective mirror and two Dinolfos inside. Jump to the side when the Dinolfos are attacking, and hit them until you defeat them. Rotate the light in the previous room, and aim it at the other light contraption. Then, go to the next room and aim the light at the bars.

Follow the light and drop into the hole. Stand on the platform with the sun on it, and reflect the light into the sun on the wall. The platform will drop and stop just in front of the large statue's head. Reflect the light onto the statue's head. Its face will fall off, but it'll reveal a target and the door to the boss room.

Longshot to the target and unlock the door to enter the boss room. You'll find Kotake, Koume, and another Iron Knuckle. Kotake and Koume will leave, and you'll need to fight against the Iron Knuckle. Defeat them like you did the previous two. The Iron Knuckle's armor will fall off, but this time you'll find out that it's actually Nabooru inside. Kotake and Koume will trap Nabooru again, and unlock the door to the next room.

Go inside, but before you climb onto the platform, there are a few things you should know about the fight against the Sorcerer Sisters, Twinrova:

For the first part of the fight, the sisters will fly around the arena, and take turns shooting a magic beam at you. Don't be afraid of their magic though, as you can reflect it with your Mirror Shield. Wait for them to use their magic, and then reflect the beam toward the other sister. If you miss the beam, there will be a patch of fire or ice (depending on which sister cast magic) on the ground. Avoid it and wait for the next magic cast. Repeat this a few times until the sisters combine into Twinrova.

For the second part of the fight, Twinrova will throw fire and ice magic at you, but you need to reflect three of the same type to counterattack. For example, if you block three ice attacks in a row, you'll reflect the attack back at Twinrova, and she'll become vulnerable. However, Twinrova is not guaranteed to cast the same type of magic three times in a row. If you've already blocked two fire attacks, but the next attack is ice, you need to dodge and wait for a fire attack. If you absorb the opposing spell, your shield will lose every charge, so make sure to dodge if the spell isn't the same type. After you reflect the magic, Twinrova will fall onto a platform. Jump over and attack her as many times as you can.

When Twinrova is casting a different type of magic, we recommend waiting on a platform off to the side of the arena, and waiting until she is just about to cast a spell. If it's the same type, you can stay on the platform and block it. If it's not the same type, jump back to the center right after she casts her spell, and her attack will land on the platform off the side. This way, the main area will be free of any ice and fire magic, and you won't have to worry about finding a safe spot to move to.

From here on out, Twinrova will incorporate a new charge attack. For the charge attack, she'll fly to the edge of the room, and quickly charge at you. Roll to the side as fast as you can to avoid her. She will cast this in between her spells, so make sure to keep the center clean of spells to ensure that you can dodge this attack.

As Twinrova is casting her spells, she'll quickly swap between her wands to trick you. She'll lift her fire wand, but then swap to her ice wand and cast ice magic instead. Sometimes, she'll swap multiple times, and other times, she'll lift the same wand multiple times in a row. It can take a while to block three of the same types of magic, so you'll need to dodge a lot. Remember to stay on a side platform to keep the center clean.

The rest of the fight will just be Twinrova either charging at you or casting a magic spell. Continue to block her attacks until you have three of the same spell type, send it back to her, and then attack as many times you can until you defeat her.

Collect the Heart Container and exit the dungeon via the ring of light as usual.

Ganondorf's Castle dungeon walkthrough in Ocarina of Time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Ganondorf's Castle is the final dungeon in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake, and you'll need to use everything you've learned from the previous dungeons to reach Ganondorf and save Princess Zelda.

There will be six rooms, with each one representing one of the elements. You'll need to solve the puzzle in each room in order to destroy the barrier protecting the central tower. However, the puzzles can get a bit tricky, and you might need a refresher on how some mechanics work.

Here's howw to complete Ganondorf's Castle and defeat him once and for all.

Head into Ganondorf's Castle, which used to be Hyrule Castle, and blow up the two Beamos down the hall. Head through the door, and you'll enter the main room inside Ganondorf's Castle. To progress, you need to destroy the six barriers blocking off the central tower. Each barrier corresponds to one of the temples. The Fire and Light doors are blocked off with black and gold blocks, so you can't go in those right away.

Before you head into any of the rooms, go under the bridge leading to the central tower. Put on the Lenses of Truth, and look at the wall to find a hidden entrance to a Fairy fountain. Come back here if you ever need to heal, or you can bottle a fairy now.

You can technically go into any of the open doors, but first, make your way to the Shadow room, which can be found behind the central pillar. We're sending you to the Shadow room first, because you'll find an important item inside.

Light the unlit torch on the pillar, and a bunch of platforms will rise from the ground. The platforms won't stay up forever, so you'll have to move quickly. If you need arrows, go to the chest on the side. If not, cross the platforms to the larger platform on the other side of the room.

Defeat the Like Like, and use your Hover Boots to cross over to the next platform. Put on the Lenses of Truth to spot a path to the button on your right. Step on it, and a chest containing the Golden Gauntlets will appear. These gauntlets will let you pick up the huge black and gold rock on the left side of the chest. Interact with the rock, and Link will automatically throw it into the pit.

Follow the path ahead and step on the button, which will unlock the door to the next room. Put on the Lenses of Truth, and you'll see a floating platform. Use your Hover Boots to reach it, and then make your way to the next room.

In this room, you'll find a mysterious bulb that is emitting dark energy. Equip the Light Arrows that Zelda gave you, and shoot an arrow at the bulb. Impa will appear and tell you that you need to save the princess, sending you back to the main room in the process.

Now, you're free to enter the rooms in any order you'd like, but first, we recommend exiting Ganon's Castle and throwing the black and gold rock just across the bridge. This will reveal the entrance to a Great Fairy Fountain, where you'll receive the Gift of Halved Damage. This will halve all damage taken, and you can get it as long as you offer 500 rupees to the fountain.

You're free to go into any room, but for this guide, we'll just be going counterclockwise from the entrance.

Head inside the Forest door, which is on your right. In this room, you'll find a Wolfos and five unlit torches (four in the center and one above the locked door). Defeat the Wolfos and use either Din's Fire or Fire Arrows to light the torches.

Go into the next room, which is a crystal room. You'll find a fan in each corner and a Beamos in the center of the room. Walk over to the left, and you'll spot a time sigil on the wall. Play the Song of Time (A - L - R- A - L - R) and a time cube will appear. Jump over to it, collect the crystal, and put on your Hover Boots before the wind blows again. Take out the Beamos, and use your Hover Boots to reach the button. Longshot to the target that rises, and collect the crystal above it. Then, use your Hover Boots to collect the remaining three crystals.

Head into the next room to find a mysterious pulsating bulb that is emitting dark energy. Equip your Fairy Bow with Light Arrows and shoot it. The barrier will fall and Saria will appear. She'll give you some motivational words before you teleport back to the main room.

Next, head into the Water room, but be careful as two Freezards will spawn as soon as you enter. If you have Fire Arrows, you can use them to melt the Freezards in one hit. Otherwise, avoid their icy breath and take them out. Break the ice stalagmites surrounding the blue fire in the center of the room. Bottle some blue fire and use it to melt the red ice. Bottle up some more blue fire for the next room. Also, don't try to open the chest on the left side of the room, because it's fake!

Go into the next room, and you'll find two movable ice blocks, like the one from the Ice Cavern. Push the farthest block to the right, into the mound of ice. Then, push it into the hole. Push the other ice block over the ice block that fell in the hole and into the ice mound. Then, push it into the other ice mound, and finally, push it toward the red ice. Melt the red ice and hit the button with the Megaton Hammer to unlock the door.

Hit the bulb with a Light Arrow, Ruto will tell you the usual spiel about helping Zelda, and you'll get teleported back to the main room.

Head to the Fire room and throw the giant black and gold rock out of the way. Equip the Goron Tunic and go inside. This is another room where you have to collect five crystals. Three can be found on the right side of the room, while the other two are on the left. However, as soon as you step onto the winding platforms, they'll all slowly sink into the lava. Quickly run to each crystal to avoid sinking. To get every crystal, you need to throw the black and gold rock into the stalactite. It'll fall into the lava and make a platform to reach one of the crystals.

After you've collected every crystal, Longshot to the door and head into the next room to find the beating bulb again. Hit it with a Light Arrow, and you'll meet your brother Darunia, who will send you back to the main room.

Throw the rock blocking the Light room, and head inside. Equip the Lenses of Truth to spot a few invisible Keese on the walls and the invisible Skulltula hanging from the ceiling. Take them out first to unlock the door and spawn a chest containing a red rupee in the center of the room. Then, spot the moving chests around the room. Don't open them, but hit them instead. Afterwards, you're free to open the other chests, which will all have a blue rupee.

Go into the next room, and use the Lenses of Truth to find the invisible chest on the left side of the room. Open it to get a small key. If you'd like a green rupee, play Zelda's Lullaby (Y - X - A - Y - X - A) on the Triforce to spawn a chest with one inside. Use the small key to open the door to the next room.

Inside this room, you need to collect all five crystals, but there are a couple rolling boulders that you need to look out for. Follow a boulder and pick up the crystals as you run around the room. After you've picked up four, stand in one of the alcoves and look at the ceiling in the center of the room. Longshot to the target, and grab the last crystal on the center platform.

Head into the next room, only to realize that the bulb is missing. A spotlight will form on top of you, meaning a Wallmaster will shortly drop onto the ground. Avoid them and take them out. Then, put on the Lenses of Truth to see the bulb through a fake wall. Go through and hit it with a Light Arrow. Rauru will appear and send you back to the main room.

Go into the Spirit room, and you'll find a bunch of statues, Blade Traps, and a Beamos. This room is also another crystal room, so you'll have to grab the five crystals to unlock the next door. Move the statues so that the Blade Traps have more space to move, which also means you'll have more time to collect the crystals. However, the statue in the far left corner is actually an Armos, so keep your Megaton Hammer ready to whack them.

After you collect the crystals, head into the next room, where you'll be greeted with two Torch Slugs in front of a mesh wall. Hit them with your Megaton Hammer to make quick work of them, and a chest containing a Business Scrub seed will appear on your right. If you look through the mesh wall, you'll see a diamond-shaped switch, and if you look above the wall, you'll see a hole. Get as close to the wall as you can, and quickly release a Bombchu. It'll crawl through the hole and blow up right next to the switch, unlocking the door to the next room.

Go to the next room, and you'll see a spider web on the ceiling and suns on the wall. Hit the spider web with a Fire Arrow or Din's Fire, and sunlight will come through the hole in the ceiling. Reflect the light onto the sun to the right of the doorway you just came through. You'll see that the sun is filling up with green light, and then the door will unlock. Don't reflect the light onto any of the suns that fill up with red light, or else a Floormaster or a Wallmaster will spawn.

Head through the unlocked door, and shoot the bulb with a Light Arrow to break the barrier.

Make your way into the central tower, and ride the elevator to the next floor. Follow the path ahead, defeat the Fire Keese, and go through the door. You'll enter a room with two Dinolfos. Wait for them to jump at you, dodge to the side, and get a few hits in. Repeat this until you defeat them both, and the door to the next room will unlock.

Follow the path to the next room, where you'll take on two Stalfos. Watch out for their sword to flash, as that means they're about to do a jump attack. Hop to the side and attack them until you defeat them both.

Head to the next room, where you'll have to take on two Iron Knuckles, but you only have to wake up one at a time. Backflip away from their side sweeping attack, and jump to the side when they slam their axe in front of them. If you do get hit by an attack, make them destroy their thrones, and three hearts will drop. After you destroy the armor on one Iron Knuckle, the other will wake up, so do your best to quickly take out the first Iron Knuckle. Once you defeat both, a chest containing the boss key will spawn.

Continue along the path, and you'll enter a room with a bunch of climbable surfaces. Go through the door and up the stairs to find the boss room door.

Before you face off against the Great Demon King, Ganondorf, there are a few things you should know about the fight:

At the start of the fight, Ganondorf will slam onto the ground, causing the floor around the center of the room to drop. Then, he'll charge up a magic orb and throw it at you. You need to hit it with your sword to reflect it back at him, but he'll also hit it back, so you'll be in a rally for a bit. After you reflect the ball three times, Ganondorf will charge up his fist and punch the ball a little harder than usual, making the ball faster. Get ready to hit the orb at a faster cadence whenever this happens.

After Ganondorf misses his return and gets hit by the orb, his chest will glow and emit dark energy, similar to the bulbs inside the rooms downstairs. Hit his chest with a Light Arrow, and he'll drop to the floor. Then, jump over to the central platform and hit Ganondorf as many times as you can.

Ganondorf will slam onto the ground again, causing a few floor tiles to fall. He'll throw another ball at you, and the fight will start to repeat. However, your rallies with Ganondorf will get longer, and he'll incorporate new attacks:

Eventually, only the floor tiles in the corners of the room will be left standing. Continue to reflect the orbs at him and avoid his other attacks until he becomes vulnerable again. Longshot to the climbable wall on the center platform, and climb up to attack him. Repeat this process until you defeat him.

After you defeat Ganondorf, Zelda will be free, but the castle will begin to collapse. Run down the stairs with Zelda either past her or by her side, and she'll eventually unlock the the door at the bottom.

Take the path to the left and make sure to jump across the gaps in the floor. You'll eventually reach another locked door that Zelda will unlock. Inside, you'll face off against two Stalfos. Defeat them and Zelda will run on ahead. Follow her and Longshot onto the target to cross the gap with Zelda.

Continue into the next room, where the floor will break, and you'll drop into the room below. Zelda will hold off the incoming enemies, and you need to find a way out. Look away from Zelda, and you'll see a rock wall on the left side of the fire. Break it to reveal a way out. Follow the path, and you'll run into a bunch of ReDead. Zelda will lock them in place with her magic. Run into the middle of the pack and do a spin attack to defeat them.

Zelda will unlock the next door, and the tower will collapse. You'll successfully make it out, but there will be a loud noise in the distance. I wonder who it might be...

Before you walk over to the pile of rubble, there are a few things you should know about the fight against Ganon:

For the first part of the fight, Ganon has one weak spot: his tail. As you don't have the Master Sword, you need to use your items to damage it, but you'll need to wait for Ganon to attack, which will give you enough time to retaliate.

Ganon has three different attacks to look out for:

After Ganon slams his sword into the ground, his sword will be stuck in the ground for a bit. Quickly run behind him and attack his tail with the Megaton Hammer or the Fairy Bow.

When Ganon lifts his sword, you can shoot an arrow between his legs to hit his tail. You'll only have a brief moment to shoot the arrow though, so be ready! Alternatively, you can try to throw a Bombchu between his legs and have it explode next to his tail. But, this is a little tricky as you'll need to time it properly.

For Ganon's jumping attack, keep some distance to avoid getting smushed. Then, wait for his next move. Continue to attack his tail until it breaks, and the fight will move on to the second phase. Zelda will throw the Master Sword back to you, but Ganon will gain a third eye and his arms will start to glow. Fortunately, his third eye will close and you'll also gain a buff of your own as the Master Sword starts to glow.

For the second part of the fight, Ganon's attacks will largely mirror his attacks from the first phase, but this time, circles will form on the ground and lightning will strike in the same location shortly after. These circles will appear as he's winding up an attack and after he swings his sword, so make sure to avoid them.

To deal damage to Ganon, you need to hit his arms with the Master Sword or Light Arrows. The only opportunity to use the Master Sword will be when Ganon's sword is stuck in the ground, so use your Light Arrows to damage his arms whenever you can. After you've dealt enough damage, his empowered arms will explode and revert to normal.

Once both of Ganon's arms have been destroyed, he'll enshroud himself in dark energy and start charging at you. Keep your distance and constantly move around the edge of the arena to avoid getting hit. Eventually, Zelda will cast magic, causing Ganon's third eye to open.

For the third part of the fight, Ganon's attacks will still be roughly the same, but now when he slams the ground, multiple ruptures will form rather than just one. However, there's a lot of space between the ruptures, so you can stand between them and you'll be safe.

Ganon's weak spot during this phase is his third eye. Shoot a Light Arrow at it whenever you can. He'll cover it just before he's about to attack, but it'll be out in the open at every other moment. After you shoot his eye three times, he'll drop to the ground, and you can attack his eye with the Master Sword, defeating him.

The Sages will use their power to seal him away, and you'll finally defeat Ganon once and for all. Watch the rest of the cutscenes, and you'll reach the end of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time.

10 Ocarina of Time beginner's tips that will save you some time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The original The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time is a tricky and difficult game, especially so when it first came out. The Ocarina of Time remake follows in the same footsteps, but just a bit more modernized. Some puzzles and quests are a tad easier now, but that doesn't mean you'll have a nice stroll through the Lost Woods. Ocarina of Time is still a challenging game that will put your brain to the test.

Here are 10 beginner's tips and tricks to help you on your journey across Hyrule.

Before you start exploring, head into your settings and adjust them to your liking. You can change how fast the camera moves, turn off motion-control aiming, swap jump from X to B, simplify your HUD, and more. It's best to change all of these settings as soon as you start playing, so you don't acclimate to the original settings.

If you didn't play the original Ocarina of Time, this may seem a bit trivial as every game has some way to sprint and jump. However, in the original, players would constantly forward roll to move faster and let the game do the jumping for them. Luckily, the Ocarina of Time remake has been modernized a tad.

To sprint, press A once and then hold it down. Unfortunately, you can't adjust it to be a toggle, but at least you don't have to mash A anymore. To jump, press X, but you can also adjust it to B in the settings. It's also important to note that you can jump attack, which will deal double damage.

When you encounter new monsters and enemies, wait for Navi to fly over them and press Right on the D-pad. Navi will tell you what the creature is, and sometimes, she'll give you tips on how to beat them or even their weakness.

As you travel to new areas in Ocarina of Time, it can be hard to figure out what you should do first to progress. Talk to the townspeople and guards, and they'll provide you with little snippets of what's happening in the area. However, sometimes they'll tell you that you should avoid certain dangerous areas, but you're the Hero of Time! Sometimes you can and should ignore the townspeoples' advice and head straight into danger.

Fairy Thoughts are, well, a fairy's thoughts scribbled onto a surface. You can find them on walls and trees all around Hyrule. Make sure to read them, because they'll tell you about secrets hidden around the area.

Like in every RPG, you'll accumulate a bunch of items. In Ocarina of Time, you'll have a bunch of bombs, Deku nuts, arrows, magic power, and more. Make sure to actually use these items rather than horde them. It's very easy to get more of these items and resources as every blade of grass, every flower, and every tree has a chance of dropping them.

As you progress through the story, you'll accumulate multiple bottles that you can store things in. Fill up your bottles with bugs, red potions, fairies, Lon Lon Milk, or literally anything that you can as you explore. You never know when you'll find a pile of soft soil that needs a bug or if you'll encounter a dire situation where you need to restore a few hearts. Additionally, if you know you're going to enter a dungeon, stock up on some healing items!

You'll come across many interesting-looking things that you can't interact with. In due time, as you complete dungeons and progress in the story, you'll unlock more key items and abilities. This does mean you'll need to revisit areas that you've already been to, but you'll be rewarded with more secrets and collectibles that are worth your while if you do.

And I mean that literally. Ocarina of Time is full of helpful sound cues. You'll hear Skulltulas and Gold Skulltulas skittering about, ominous noises when an enemy is targeting you, icicles cracking just as they're about to fall, and, most importantly, delightful music in each area. The sounds you hear will help you progress through dungeons and solve difficult puzzles.

Zelda Notes is a feature on the Nintendo Switch App that has quizzes, your play data, the Threads of Time, and even additional quests. However, the most useful feature is its map, as it'll track your location in game in real time and show you the locations of collectibles.

Before you use it though, it's important to note that the app can and will show you spoilers for Ocarina of Time, so it might be best to play through the game first, especially if it's your first time playing. Whenever you're ready to use Zelda Notes, make sure to head to the Settings and enable it. Then, download the Nintendo Switch App on your mobile device, and link your account. As soon as you walk out of your house, the app will be good to go.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

All bottle locations in Ocarina of Time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Store all of your valuables... like this bug!

Bottles are one of the many items that Link carries around in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake.

Bottles are extremely valuable, as they allow you to store things like potions, fairies, bugs, and Lon Lon Milk. You can use your stored items at any time you wish, meaning you can drink your red potion or Lon Lon milk if you're just about to die. However, bottles can be hard to come by, as you'll need to go a bit out of your way to find them.

Here's where to find every bottle in Ocarina of Time remake.

In Ocarina of Time remake, you can have up to five bottles at once, which is one more than in the original game.

You can find a bottle in the following locations:

For more information on how to get every bottle, check out the sections below.

You can get the first bottle at the Potion Shop in Hyrule Castle Town. On your first visit to the shop, the Potion Shopkeeper will be panicking about a bug that escaped. They'll give you an empty bottle to catch the bug skittering around. Press R and swap to the empty bottle. Then, walk over to the bug on the floor and press ZR to scoop it up. As a reward, he'll give you a red potion. If you drink the potion, it'll just turn into an empty bottle, which you can use to store other things like another bug.

After you wake up Talon outside of Hyrule Castle, he'll run back home to Lon Lon Ranch. Go to his house during the day, and you'll find him asleep on the floor. Talk to him and he'll ask if you want to play his cucco-findin' game, where you have to find three super Cuccos within 30 seconds.

Before you start, look at the three Cuccos behind Talon, which are the three super Cuccos. They'll periodically lift their wings into the air and let out a cuckoo. To find the super Cuccos during the game, you'll need to quickly look around the room to find the three Cuccos that do the same action, and pick them up.

When you're ready to start, pay Talon 10 rupees, and watch the super Cuccos fly away behind him. The direction they go is indicative of where they are in the room. To make things easier, each super Cucco flies farther than every other Cucco, so you'll find them in their respective corners of the house. Quickly look to the southwest corner, northwest corner, and northeast corner, spot the Cucco that lifts their wings, and pick up them. Additionally, after the super Cuccos fly away, they'll always start off facing the wall.

If you complete the challenge, you'll earn a bottle full of Lon Lon Milk. If you fail, you can immediately retry for five rupees, but if you leave and come back, you'll need to pay full price.

For those who have played the original, you unfortunately cannot throw the Cuccos away before the game starts, so you'll need to find them the hard way.

The third bottle is given to you by the Cucco Lady in Kakariko Village. After you talk to her, you'll learn that she's missing all seven of her Cuccos, and it's up to you to return them to their pen. Every Cucco can be found in Kakariko Village, but some of them are a bit trickier to retrieve.

In the map above, we've marked the locations of every missing Cucco, but the Cuccos do like to roam around a bit. Well, except for Cucco #2 as they're trapped in a box. For Cucco #5, 6, and 7, you'll need to use another Cucco to reach them. After you return every Cucco to the pen, the Cucco Lady will give you an empty bottle.

The fourth bottle is found in Lake Hylia. First, progress through the story until you unlock the Silver Scale at the Zora Domain. Then, dive into the shortcut that takes you from the Zora Domain to Lake Hylia. If you look underwater in front of you, you'll see a bottle that has a bunch of air bubbles coming out of it. Dive into the water and grab it to find Ruto's Letter, but more importantly, a bottle!

The fifth bottle is the hardest to get out of all the bottles. To get this one, you'll need to hunt down five Big Poes as adult Link. Look out for the lanterns scattered around Hyrule Field and jump over them with Epona. You can find the lantern and Big Poe locations in the map above.

A Big Poe will spawn, and you need to hunt them down. You can either hit them with two arrows or one Light Arrow, and they'll turn into a yellow ghost orb. Bottle them up, and take them to the Poe Collector, who has made the old guard room in Hyrule Castle Town his storefront.

As soon as you enter Hyrule Castle Town, turn right, and you'll find the entrance to his shop. Sell five Big Poes to him for 30 rupees each, and he'll give you 500 points and an empty bottle. If you turn in all 10 Big Poes, they'll reward you with a gold rupee, which is worth 200 rupees!

The Christmas countdown is officially underway, and if you’re anything like me, a few festive movies and shows are essential for getting into the holiday spirit. Thankfully, Netflix is making it easier to get started, with the streaming service adding a fresh batch of Christmas movies throughout November. Whether you’re ready for cozy festive romances, family-friendly adventures or something a little different to break up the usual holiday viewing, there’s plenty to watch.

With new originals joining some overlooked Christmas gems, this month’s lineup has a little something for different tastes. It’s also a good excuse to revisit your festive watchlist and discover a few new favorites before December rolls around. I’ve picked out five Christmas movies and shows I’d recommend checking out first, so you can get your holiday viewing off to a good start.

“The Best Christmas Pageant Ever” is a charming comedy-drama based on Barbara Robinson’s much-loved 1972 children’s novel. Directed by Dallas Jenkins, best known for creating the hit series “The Chosen,” the movie was a passion project more than 15 years in the making, with Jenkins describing the book as an important part of his childhood. Released in November 2024, it earned positive reviews from critics and audiences, with Judy Greer and Beatrice Schneider among the standout performers. It’s a lovely choice for a family movie night.

Six unruly siblings known as the Herdmans have a reputation for causing trouble wherever they go. When they turn up at the local church looking for free snacks, they end up volunteering for the annual Christmas pageant and landing all the leading roles. Grace Bradley (Judy Greer), who has taken over directing the production for the first time, is left scrambling to prepare the children for their big performance. With the Herdmans unfamiliar with the Christmas story and the rest of the town convinced the show will be a disaster, rehearsals quickly become unpredictable.

“No Sleep ’Til Christmas” is a cozy holiday rom-com that first premiered on Freeform as part of its annual “25 Days of Christmas” lineup. The movie puts a fun spin on the festive formula with a quirky premise centred on two strangers struggling with severe insomnia who discover they can only fall asleep when they’re together. Dave Annable and Odette Annable lead the cast, and their real-life marriage brings an easy chemistry to the central relationship. It’s a lighthearted pick if you’re after a feel-good Christmas movie with a slightly unusual twist.

Lizzie (Odette Annable) is a successful event planner struggling with insomnia just weeks before her wedding to fiancé Josh (Charles Michael Davis). One night, she has an unexpected encounter with Billy (Dave Annable), a bartender who is also having trouble sleeping. After discovering they can both drift off peacefully when lying next to each other, Lizzie suggests an unusual arrangement. She offers to help Billy fund his dream of opening a bar in exchange for keeping her company at night. However, their increasingly complicated arrangement starts to interfere with Lizzie’s wedding plans.

Netflix is kicking off its original holiday movie lineup with “A Dog’s Perfect Christmas,” a family drama based on W. Bruce Cameron’s 2019 novel. The story follows a family navigating a difficult festive season, with plenty of emotional moments alongside the usual Christmas cheer. Cameron co-wrote the screenplay with director Cathryn Michon, continuing their creative partnership after working together on “A Dog’s Purpose” and “A Dog’s Journey.” This is perfect for anyone who enjoys heartfelt animal movies and festive family stories.

Widowed grandfather Sander Goss (Dennis Quaid) is struggling to get along with his teenage granddaughter Ello (Brooke Lena Johnson) two years after losing his wife. When a sudden medical emergency leaves Ello’s mother in an induced coma just before Christmas, the family is thrown into turmoil. With the household struggling to cope, their loyal basset hound, Winstead (voiced by Thomas Haden Church), takes matters into his own paws. Alongside an energetic Corgi puppy named Ruby, Winstead sets out to bring the family together and help them prepare for Juliana’s return.

If you’d rather binge-watch a cozy mystery over the festive season, “Mistletoe Murders” is a great choice, especially with the series already climbing Netflix’s top 10. It originally began as an Audible audio drama in 2021, starring Cobie Smulders, before making the jump to television for Hallmark+ and the Hallmark Channel. The story brings a festive twist to the cozy mystery formula, pairing a charming small-town setting and plenty of Christmas decorations with a series of murder investigations. There’s nothing wrong with your holiday viewing having a little crime and suspense.

Emily Lane (Sarah Drew) runs Under the Mistletoe, a Christmas-themed gift shop in the small town of Fletcher’s Grove. Although she appears to be an ordinary shop owner, Emily has a mysterious past and a set of unusual skills that make her particularly good at investigating crimes. When a local murder puts one of her new friends under suspicion, Emily takes matters into her own hands to uncover the truth. Along the way, she crosses paths with Detective Sam Wilner (Peter Mooney), whose own investigations bring them closer together while raising questions about Emily’s real identity.

Another major Netflix original arriving this month is “Guarding Stars,” a festive romantic comedy based on Katherine Center’s bestselling novel “The Bodyguard.” The story follows a highly trained female protection agent who is assigned to look after a famous Hollywood actor, turning the usual action-romance formula on its head. Leighton Meester, best known for “Gossip Girl,” and Jared Padalecki of “Supernatural” and “Gilmore Girls” lead the cast, giving fans of both stars another reason to tune in. If you enjoy fun holiday romances with a little action and plenty of familiar faces, this one is worth adding to your watchlist.

Hannah Brooks (Leighton Meester) is a no-nonsense professional bodyguard assigned to protect Jack Stapleton (Jared Padalecki), a famous action movie star, during the Christmas holidays. When Jack returns to his family’s Montana ranch, Hannah accompanies him to keep him safe from an unwanted threat. To avoid raising suspicions about her real job, she agrees to pose as his girlfriend while staying with his family. However, as they spend more time together, their pretend relationship becomes increasingly complicated.

Media Briefing: Publishers’ revenue growth defies traffic declines, but tougher comparisons loom

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This Media Briefing covers the latest in media trends for Digiday+ members and is distributed over email every Thursday at 10 a.m. ET. More from the series →

This week’s Media Briefing looks at how The New York Times, People Inc., News Corp and Reuters are navigating tougher year-over-year revenue comparisons after reporting strong growth despite declining referral traffic and AI disruption.

Declining referral traffic and AI disruption aren’t stopping some of the largest publishers from growing revenue, according to recent earnings. The New York Times, News Corp, People Inc., Reuters all reported gains in the third quarter, fueled by a mix of subscriptions, advertising and content licensing.

But their latest earnings also point to a common challenge heading into the final part of the year: sustaining that momentum as last year’s revenue gains make for tougher comparisons.

Notably, AI licensing has delivered real revenue growth for People Inc. and Reuters, especially as they look to diversify revenue beyond advertising. But the initial boost from those deals is becoming harder to replicate as those agreements mature.

“The short-term revenue win that publishers got by striking LLM deals helps for a quarter or four to eight, but it’s often at the expense of lost audience and inventory,” said Matt Prohaska, CEO and principal of Prohaska Consulting.

The New York Times is also bracing for tougher year-over-year comparisons in the fourth quarter, although in its case, the challenge stems from an advertising supply increase in 2025.

Meanwhile, News Corp’s latest earnings painted a more mixed picture, revealing publishers face different levels of exposure to declining audiences and traditional advertising pressures, even within the same company. The company posted strong revenue growth in its Dow Jones business, amid declines in its News Media division.

Digiday broke down what media executives said in their earnings call this week so far (still a few more to go, with USA Today Co., BuzzFeed and Ziff Davis later this week), and what it reveals about where publishers see their businesses heading next:

What was said: “The comps get tougher as the year goes on. Q4 is our biggest quarter in ads this year and last year. I’ll remind you we grew 25%. And last year we benefited from these sort of structural supply increases in certain parts of the portfolio.” – Meredith Kopit Levien, CEO, The New York Times Company.

What it really means: The Times benefited from a significant expansion of ad inventory last year, helping drive 25% YOY growth in digital advertising in Q4 2025. That sets a high baseline for the same quarter this year, making it more difficult to sustain that pace of growth, Kopit Levien said in the earnings call on Nov. 4. Digital advertising revenue growth has already slowed, from 20.7% YOY in Q2 2026 to 12% in Q3.

Because of this, the Times expects more modest advertising growth this quarter – though Kopit Levien said in the earnings call that the company will continue to add supply going forward, though it will be “more incremental and more across the portfolio.”

What was said: “The licensing revenue, as I said and as we saw, was really impressive. We continue to have real success across Apple News, AI, and other content partnerships. In Q4, those growth rates will slow some as we lap the Meta AI deal that we announced last Q4. We still expect full-year growth to be about what we delivered in licensing in the first half which translates to something roughly about 20% Q4 growth. Again, our focus is to get back to the growth we want and expect.” – Tim Quinn, CFO, People Inc.

What it really means: People Inc. has three publicly disclosed AI licensing deals, with OpenAI, Meta and Microsoft. The Meta deal Quinn referenced during the Nov. 3 earnings call was signed last December. While People Inc.’s licensing revenue grew 49% year over year in Q3 2026, Quinn cautioned investors against expecting a similar growth rate in the fourth quarter, as the company begins to lap the initial revenue boost from its Meta deal.

But People Inc. execs remained optimistic about the AI licensing market. “They will continue to evolve and there’s a big place for our content. There’s increasingly a place for recency — meaning new content — in that marketplace,” Quinn said in the earnings call.

However, the majority of People Inc.’s licensing revenue growth came from non-AI content licensing deals. Apple News has been a “great success,” Quinn said, alongside distribution deals with platforms such as Yahoo and MSN. In total, about 15 of People Inc.’s brands are successfully generating content licensing revenue, Quinn added. Overall, People Inc.’s digital revenue grew 2% YOY in Q3 2026.

What was said: “We expect Reuters growth to be flattish year-over-year in the fourth quarter due to lapping $5 million of transactional AI licensing revenue from the prior year period, along with tougher comps at agency and in portions of the professional business.”  – Gary Bischoping, CFO, Thomson Reuters.

What it really means: Reuters also has three publicly disclosed AI content licensing deals: with Meta and Microsoft in October 2024, and a deal with Amazon in May 2026. However, a Reuters executive previously told Digiday that the company has other AI content licensing deals that it has not spoken about publicly, and cited the company’s earnings as a sign of this. The full list of Reuters’ AI licensing partners and the number of deals is not clear. But what is clear is that revenue from these deals has helped boost Reuters’ overall business.

Reuters organic revenue grew 12% in Q3 2026, thanks to about $19 million of transactional generative AI content licensing in the quarter, per Bischoping. When excluding transactional AI licensing revenue in the third quarter of both 2026 and 2025, Reuters’ revenue grew 7% YOY. While AI licensing was a meaningful contributor to Reuters’ growth this past quarter, it can create volatility in year-over-year comparisons, as its not recurring revenue for the company.

Also, stronger prior-year performance in Reuters’ agency and professional businesses will mean tougher comps in Q4 2026, Bischoping said.

What was said: “News Media continues to navigate complex market pressures, including a challenging advertising environment and the ongoing evolution of the search and social algorithm landscape. As mentioned earlier, that search landscape will change dramatically with the advent of agentic AI. Revenues for the segment declined slightly versus prior year to $540 million.” – Robert Thomson, CEO, News Corp

What it really means: Revenue at News Corp’s Dow Jones business — which includes titles like The Wall Street Journal, Barron’s and MarketWatch — grew 8% year over year, fueled by subscriptions, licensing and its professional information businesses, according to its earnings released on Nov. 4. Digital advertising revenue increased 12%, matching the growth rate reported by The New York Times in Q3. These results are an example of how large publishers are finding ways to grow revenue beyond traditional traffic-dependent business models, even as some face tougher year-over-year comparisons ahead.

However, revenue at the company’s News Media division — which includes The Sun, The Times of London and the New York Post — declined 1% YOY. The division’s ad revenue fell 2% YOY, despite gains in subscription pricing and content licensing revenue. Audience declines were also evident: The Sun’s global monthly unique users fell 26% YOY in September, while the New York Post’s digital audience declined 11%.

“I think this is useless and is marketing, and far, far, far too late… It does nothing and doesn’t solve the problem at all. And so it’s purely window dressing.”

– Paul Bannister, chief strategy officer at Raptive, on the robots.txt “policy token” multiple AI companies have agreed to honor, which they say will allow publishers to opt out of having their content used to train generative AI models.

15%: The YOY growth of People Inc.’s non-session-based revenue in Q3 2026, due to strength in licensing, content syndication, events and social video. This now makes up 43% of People Inc.’s overall digital revenue.

26%: The reduction in unique visitors to participating Digital Content Next members’ sites from 2023 to 2025, according to data collected by the publisher trade group and included in an amicus brief filed in a copyright lawsuit against OpenAI and Microsoft.

38%: The share of people using AI to find information weekly, up from 11% in 2024, making it the main use of AI, according to the Reuters Institute for the Study of Journalism’s “Generative AI and News Report 2026.”

51%: The share of people using AI tools weekly in 2026, nearly triple the 18% reported in 2024, according to the Reuters Institute report.

People Inc. builds custom APIs to make its journalism easier for AI agents to access

Giggly Squad’s $160 million SiriusXM deal signals a shift from podcast networks to creator ecosystems

OpenAI, Google and Microsoft back new standard for publishers to block AI scraping

OpenAI, Google, Microsoft and Anthropic have agreed to support a new robots.txt “policy token” standard that would allow publishers to opt out of their content being scraped and used for AI training, without needing to configure restrictions for each one individually, The Verge reported.

Condé Nast faces $35 million ad revenue shortfall

Condé Nast is reportedly on track to miss its Q4 U.S. advertising target by more than $35 million, amid senior sales departures and uncertainty over its commercial strategy, Adweek reported.

People Inc. chief executive Neil Vogel told staff in a memo that the company will cut 195 people, as a result of the “rapid changes in media,” The New York Times reported.

New York Times and other publishers sue Brave over AI scraping

The New York Times and several other newspapers (including the Daily News, the Chicago Tribune, and the Orlando Sentinel) have sued Brave, accusing the web browser company of illegally scraping their content for AI platforms and allowing users to bypass their paywalls, Reuters reported.

Bloomberg to pay $500,000 over misleading subscription practices

DoorDash’s announced plans to acquire Instacart for $17 billion point to the constant need to gain market share by diversifying services and offerings.

Publishers are pushing back against Meta’s AI scraping terms, testing their leverage over a platform they still rely on.
